1. PURPOSE
1.1 This fictional SOP defines how FTD logs, classifies, investigates and closes deviations from
    approved procedures, specifications or planned conditions.

2. SCOPE
2.1 Applies to all FTD departments: Manufacturing, Quality Control, Warehouse, Engineering,
    Quality Assurance, Packaging and Facilities.
2.2 Temperature excursions are a special deviation type handled by FTD-SOP-QA-0101; if an
    excursion also reveals a procedural failure, a deviation is raised in addition.

3. DEFINITIONS
3.1 Deviation: any unplanned departure from an approved instruction, specification or condition.
3.2 Discovery date: the date the deviation was first noticed.
3.3 Logged date: the date the deviation was entered in the deviation system.
3.4 CAPA: Corrective and Preventive Action, with ID format CAPA-YYYY-NNNN.
3.5 Overdue: a deviation whose days open (or days to close) exceed its closure target.

4. RESPONSIBILITIES
4.1 Reporter (any employee): reports the deviation to their supervisor and QA without delay.
4.2 Department Head: ensures the deviation is logged and provides subject-matter input.
4.3 QA Reviewer: assigns the class, approves the investigation plan and approves closure.
4.4 QA Head: approves closure of MAJOR and CRITICAL deviations and approves CAPAs.

5. CATEGORIES AND CLASSIFICATION
5.1 Deviation categories: EQUIPMENT, PROCESS, DOCUMENTATION, MATERIAL, PERSONNEL, ENVIRONMENTAL, FACILITY.
5.2 Class is assigned from impact assessment:
      MINOR    : Product_Impact = NONE
      MAJOR    : Product_Impact = POTENTIAL
      CRITICAL : Product_Impact = CONFIRMED, OR any patient-safety or data-integrity concern
                 (regardless of Product_Impact)
5.3 Root cause categories: HUMAN_ERROR, EQUIPMENT_FAILURE, PROCEDURE_INADEQUATE, MATERIAL_DEFECT,
    TRAINING_GAP, ENVIRONMENTAL_CONDITION, UNKNOWN.

6. PROCEDURE
6.1 Report and log
    6.1.1 The deviation must be logged within 1 calendar day of discovery (LogDelay_days <= 1).
          A deviation logged more than 1 day after discovery is recorded as "Logged_In_SLA = N".
    6.1.2 QA assigns a Deviation ID in the format DEV-YYYY-NNNN, where YYYY is the year of the
          logged date. The sequence restarts each year.
6.2 Initial assessment
    6.2.1 QA Reviewer completes the impact assessment and assigns the class within 2 business days
          of logging.
    6.2.2 Immediate containment actions are taken and documented for MAJOR and CRITICAL deviations.
6.3 Investigation
    6.3.1 The investigation identifies the root cause category (Section 5.3) and documents evidence.
    6.3.2 Closure target (calendar days from logged date to closure):
            MINOR : 30 days     MAJOR : 45 days     CRITICAL : 60 days
6.4 CAPA
    6.4.1 A CAPA is REQUIRED for every MAJOR and CRITICAL deviation.
    6.4.2 A CAPA is OPTIONAL for MINOR deviations and raised when a recurrence trend is found.
6.5 Closure
    6.5.1 MINOR deviations are closed by the QA Reviewer.
    6.5.2 MAJOR and CRITICAL deviations are closed by the QA Head.
    6.5.3 A deviation still open when days since logging exceed its closure target is flagged
          Overdue = Y; a closed deviation whose days to close exceeded the target is also Overdue = Y.
6.6 Trending
    6.6.1 QA reviews the deviation log monthly for recurring categories and root causes.

7. RECORDS AND RETENTION
7.1 Deviation records, investigations and CAPA records are retained for 7 years.

8. REVISION HISTORY
    Rev 01 | 2022-11-15 | Initial issue
    Rev 02 | 2025-06-01 | Added patient-safety / data-integrity rule for CRITICAL; added closure
             targets of 30 / 45 / 60 days; logging deadline set to 1 calendar day
